wind arrived at the threshold and looking archly said thus: “ O Lord and

CHAPTER III. 


1-4. Siva said, “O Parvati, listen and you will be blessed. Every 
aci, in the world, is accomplished by devices. I am going to suggest to 
you a pleasing method which is the root of success. O fair one, adore Hari 
and perform a holy vow. The name of that vow is Pünyaka. It should 
be performed only fora year. Though it is austere, yet it is efficacious like 
the Kalpa treo. It is pleasing, sanctifying, substantial, excellent and calculated 
to give a son and all prosperity.

31-38. O blessed Parvati, perform this vow and you will obtain a son. 
Lord Krisna, the source of all bliss through whom millions of ancestors of a 
person are redeemed is worshipped by this vow. Whoever takes the 
Mantra of Hari and serves him blesses his own existence, redeems his ancestors, 
goes to the Vaikuntha and dwells there as a member of the retinue of Lord 
Krisna. He redeems his brother, servant, friend, companion and wife and 
merges into the feet of Hari. Therefore, O Parvati, take that rare mantra of 
Hari and recite it.” So saying, O saint, S’iva, the primeval god, went with 
his spouse to the coast of the Ganges, gave her the mantra of Hari, his ode 
and amulet and pointed out to her the methods of celebrating his worship.

13-62. O goddess, I am going to relate to you the method of the worship 
and everything, as desired by you. With a view to the collection of fruits and 
flowers for the vow, a hundred Brahmins should be engaged. To provide 
articles, a hundred servants should be employed. You should engage the wise, the 
learned, the pious, the all-knowing Sanat-Kumára as your priest. My dear, the 
initiation of the rite in the month of Mágha in the bright half of the lunar month 
on the 13th day of the said month is most auspicious. On the previous day, 
the person intent on her vow, should shave hor head, bathe, fast and carefully 
wash her clothes Next day, she should got up at dawn, rinse her mouth and 
bathe. in pure water. After recollecting Hari, she should utter the mantra for 
the purification of the body. ‘hen reverentially she should offer oblations to 
the feet of Hari and go home. She should put on a pair of washed clothes, sit 
down on a clean matting, rinse her monthagain, mark her forehead with sandal 
paste and perform her diurnal rites. First she should greet the priest, utter Swasti 
(the ceremony preparatory to a solemn sacrifice) fix the jar, make the avowal 
of the purpose to perform the rite and then commence the vow, as prescribed by 
the Vedas. She should daily present 16 ingredients to Lord Visnu, viz., the 
expression Swágata (welcome), water for washing the feet, offerings (barley eto.) 
for the feet, water torinse the mouth, oblations of milk and honey, water to 
bathe, cloth and ornaments, perfume, flower, frankincense, light, sacrificial offer- 
ings (rice, etc.), sacred thread, betel incensed with camphor and hymn. 


27-36. If any of the above articles be wanting, the act is defective. A 
defective act is like a defective man. A defective act brings forth defective 
fruits. The worshipper, to gain a graceful form, should present every day 
one hundred and eight Párijat flowers to Visnu. To add to the beauty of her 
person, she should offer a lakh of white Champaka flowers to Visnu. To add 
to the beauty of her face, she should present to him a like number of lotuses. 
To add to the lustre of her eyes, she should offer him a thousand mirrors 
(looking glasses) and for the grace of her eyes, she should offer him a lakh 
of blue lotuses. or the beauty of her hair, she should present to him a 
hundred white chowrios. For the beauty of her nose, she should offer him a 
thousand cups made of leaves. For the beauty of her lips, she should offer 
him a lakh of red flowers called Bandhuka. [or the beauty of her teeth, she 
should offer him one lakh pearls. For the beauty of her cheek, she should | 
offer him one lakh pillows inlaid with gems.

CHAPTER V. 


1-12. Náráyana said, Dürgà having learnt from S'iva the method of per- 
forming the vow again queried him anent this wonderful subject thus :—'* Lord, 
what an amazing subject I have heacd. Now describe to me everything con- 
nected with the vow. Please tell me by whom it was first performed." 
"Siva said: ** S'atarup&, the barren wife of Manu, afflicted in her heart for want of 
s son, went to Brahmá and said: “ O lord, you are the author of the universe: 
please tell me how a barren woman may get a son. Alas my life and prospe- 
rity are in —effectual. "The (useless) merits acquired by asceticism and gift bear 
fruits which are reaped only in the next world. But a son confers upon 
his parents immediate happiness, redemption and fruits of a hundred 
royal sacrifices, and saves them from the peril of the hell called Puta. 
Lord, tell me how to get a son; otherwise I will resort to the jungles. 
Please take away all our kingdom, wealth and prosperity which are useless to 
us (me and my husband) without a son. A learned manis not pleased with 
the sight of a man who is sonless and who is ashamed of even showing his own 
face to the world. Unless I get a son, I will take poison or enter into the 
flames. You had better remain with your unworthy son who has got no son 
and who will be denuded of a wife by my suicide.” So saying, she wept with 
grief. The merciful Brahma thereupon said : 


13-29. Child, I am going to tell you an auspicious and delightful 
method adapted to give a person’ all sorts of prosperity and fulfil all 
kinds of desire including the birth of a son. You should perform the 
Punyaka vow which should be observed in the bright half of the lunar 
month on the thirteenth day of the said month. Krisna is worshipped by this 
vow which lasts for a year. All things prescribed by the Vedas are offered 
to Him in the course of this vow. You will get a son valorous like Visnu 
by the performance of this vow, as stated by Kánwa Sakha.” Thereupon . 
S'atarupá performed the vow and got two sons named Priya-vrata and Uttánpáda.

six months pass, it is increased to an amount four times the amount specified 
last; if a year passes, the rites are ineffectual. In the last case, the 
worshipper (the person who performs the rites) suffers the torments of 
hell for a thousand years. He loses his son, grandson, wealth, prosperity 
and virtuo.”

as a Being vested with four hands, as the lovely consort of Laksmi. ‘The 
Vaisnavas regard you as corporeal vested with a lovely form, holding ia your 
hands conches, wheel, mace and lotus. Your votaries contemplate you as 
possessed of two hands, lovely, young, dark-blue in appearance, tranquil, 
husband of the cow-berdess decorated with gems and yet effulgent. 
Had you not been effulgent, why should the Yogis have meditated you 
at all? I was adored in days of old by Brahma with a view to the 
destruction of the demons: aud so I emanated fron the valour of the gods, I 
am eternal and represent your lustre, and yet L manifested myself in the form of 
a woman. I represent your illusion’ by which I was bewildered, killed the 
demons, and then went to the Himalayas. 


121-131. Later on, when the gods were oppressed by the demon called 
T&rakáksya I was born as the daughter of Dakga and married to S'iva, at the 
instance of Brahma, and others. In this birth, also, I got as my husband 
S'iva who married me, as he was pleased with my devotions. But being bewild- 
erd by the gods, I was unable to sustain in my womb the semen of S/iva. 
O god, Ladored you, therefore, to obtain a son. By means of this vow, I 
desire a son like yourself. The Vedas prescribe to the worshipper, O Lord, her 
husband as the priest’s fee on the occasion of this vow. O ocean of mercy, 
have compassion on me for the above reasons." So saying, O Nárada, Párvati 
paused. Whoever with a pure heart listens to the above ode gets a son valorous 
like Visnu. A person who eats only ghee and boiled rice for a year and for this 
period worships Hari reaps the benefit of the Punyaka vow. This excellent 
hymn of Visnu gives prc sperity, happiness and redemption to a woman, secure 
io her the good graces of her husband and contributes to her beauty and fame. 
It produces reverence for Hari, developes the knowledge of ontology and gives 
vigour to the intellect.

CHAPTER XII. 


1.7. Náráyana said, *Durgá having heard this, remembered Hari and 
said, ‘The whole world is subservient to the will of God." A victim to predes- 
tination, she was prompted by curiosity and said to Saturn, “Look at me and 
my son." Who can resist the dispensation of fate? Saturn, when he heard 
Parvati, cogitated in his mind as to whether he should see Párvati's son or 
not. ‘Then having cited Dharma as his witness, he desired to see the child but 
not his mother. Before he saw him, however, his mind was very much dejected 
and his throat, lipsand palate were dried up. From the corner of his left eye 
he beheld the son's face whereupon the head of the boy was at once severed 
from his body. S/ani at once turned away his face and hung down his head. 
The headless trunk of the boy besmeared with blood entered into the lap of 
Párvati and his head passed to the Goloka and merged into Lord Krisna.

1-19. Narayana said, '*Kártika having said so addressed the Krittikás thus, 
using words which were full of ethical principles: —‘ I am going to S’iva to see 
the gods, my mother and friends. Adieu! The world, the Karma, birth, associa- 
tion and separation are all subject to the all-powerful Fate. But Fate itself 
is subject to Krişņa and therefore savants worship the Suprme Lord. 
Krisna alone can strengthen or weaken the hands of Fate. The worshipper of 
Krisna isalso not controlled by Fate. The Sástras hold that he is immortal. 
Therefore I request you io give up the ties of affection and adore Hari who 
bestows happiness and beautitude and removes the fear of death and rebirth. 
Even Brahma, Visnu and Mahesa always minister to Lord Krisna who cuts 
asunder the ties of worldly attachments. In this sea of the world, what am I to 
you and what are you to us? What relation subsists between you‘and me? As 
currents of water accumulate in the sea, so we have assembled here like currents 
of time. Union and separation depend on the will of God. ` Savants regard 
this universe as subject to the will of God and by no means free. These three 
worlds are unreal like bubbles of water. Bewildered fools alone are attached to 
the transitory objects of the world. The worshippers of Krisna never mingle 
with any thing and areas free as air. O mothers, get over your attachment 
and part with me." So saying he bowed to the Krittikás, recollected Hari and

CHAPTER XXI 


1-10. Nárada said, “How, O lord, did the gods, cursed bya Br&hmin, 
lose their prosperity? How did they regain their fortune?" Narayana said, 
“The foolish Indra in his misfortune being deserted by Rambhá and the 
elephant went back to his capital. He saw that Amaráyati was teeming with his 
enemies and void of his friends. It presented a very sorry and miserable 
appearance. Having learnt every thing from his messengers, he went to his 
religious preceptor at first and then to Brahmá along with his preceptor and 
the gods. Indra bowed to Brahma and worshipped him according to the rites 
prescribed by the Vedas. Brahm, when he learnt every thing from Vrihas- 
pati; hung down his head and said, “Indra, you are my grandson and by the 
grace of fortune you have been effulgent. Your wife Sachi is like Laksmi; 
and yet you court other females. In days of old, your body was filled with 
vaginas by the curse of Gautama, Again you have co-habited with another 
nymph. One who courts the wife of another person forfeits one’s fortune, fame 
and reputation in society. ree 


11-19. The saint Durvâså gave you the flower dedicated to Hari. But 
as your mind was set upon Rambha, you cast it on the head of your elephant, 
Air&vata. Now where is Rambhá,a public whore? And where are yon now, . 
O king of the gods ? You lost your prosperity. So Laksmi quitted you ina 
moment; and so did Rambhá. A prostitute wants a wealthy and young lover. 
She slanders the old. © son, no one can check the inevitable. Now, in order 
to gain back your fortune (Laksmi), you should adore Hari.” So saying he 
gave Indra the amulet, hymn and incantations of Náráyana. Indra then 
departed with his guru and the gods and went to Puskara to adore Hari. He 
served him fora year and fasted all along. Hari appeared to him, gave him 
the necessary boon and the mantras and went back to the Vaikuntha. Indra : 
went to the sea of milk, wielded the amulet and obtained Kamala through 
adorations and hymns. He then subdued his foes and regained his kingdom. 
The gods also secured their former position. pe -

20-30. The following meditation sacred to the goddess which is abstruse, 
attainable by the Munis with very great difficulty and which is productive of all 
prosperity is mentioned in the Sám-Veda. Her colour is like the white 
champaká: her grace equals the charm of a hundred moons; sho is clad in dress 
chastened by the heat of fire; she is decorated with ornaments; she has a 
smiling, cheerful aspect; she is kind to her votaries, Her forehead is marked 
with vermilion and musk; she is decked with precious ear-rings; she wields on. 
her head the load of a chignon covered with jasmine wreath. Sho is seated merrily 
on thé lotus of a thousand petals. You should adore the spouse of Hari who 
enchants the heart and is vested with the attribute of tranquillity and who is” 
the mother of the universe. Then, you should offer her, O Indra, sixteen kinds 
of ingredients. After having worshipped her, with the following hymn, you. 
will obtain the boon desired. "The mysterious, delightful hymn is as follows: 
“O goddess, I want to adore you; but I am unable to.do so. You are a goddess 
beyond comprehension, eternal like the subtle Light and indescribable. O 
mother, you can accomplish anything that you desire; you have no form; you | 
only take a form to favour your' worshippers; you are imperceptible to the 
‘mind and baffle all powers of description. How can I adore you? You are the. 
limit or the extremity of the four Vedas and the pilot in this sea of the world, 
the mistress of the crops and the source of all prosperity. 


31-40. You are the mother of asceticism, ascetics, wisdom, the savants, 
the Vedas and the people versed in the Vedas. How can I describe you? As to 
an infant which sucks the breast of ils mother, everything is non-existent without 
its mother, so the whole world is nil without you. But conjoined with you it

tence. Let the Brahmins be at first satisfied and give me the necessary

houses full of friends and free from enemies. There was a beat of drum in the 
heaven; and there was a rain of flowers from the sky. The gods regained their 
kingdom and fortune. Thus I have described to you the excellent, edifying, 
redeeming character of Laksmi. What more do you want to hear ?

21-30. When Jamadagni secured all the necessary articles, he easily 


feasted the king and hisarmy. The king, when he witnessed the abundant 
supply of rare articles, was surprised and said to his minister, ‘‘Look here: 
objects which are unheard of and rare have been secured in this place. Please 
ascertain how." The minister enquired into the matter and said to the king, 
“O king, lam going to inform your majesty what I saw in the house of the 
saint. His house contains sacrificial pits, fuels for sacrifice, flowers, fruits, hides 
of black antelopes, garlands, and wreaths of flowers and teems with springs and 


his disciples. It does not contain gold, crops or wealth. His wives have no. 


ornaments and simply put on barks of trees. His sons put on the crust of trees 
and clusters of hair. But in a corner of his house, there is a Kapila or celestial 
cow that is lovely, having the colour of the moonlight, with eyes like the red- 
lotus, effulgent with her own lustre like the full Moon, looking like the asylum 
of all prosperity and virtue and personification of Laksmi herself.” Thereupon 
the foolish king, entangled in the meshes of time,(t. e. like one whose days were 
numbered) demanded Kapila from the saint, as advised by his minister. 


31-40. O Narada, Fate is more powerful thaa virtue, wisdom or anything, 
for even the wise and virtuous king demanded the cow from the Muni. In India 
food and holy deeds are the outcome of merits acquired by a person in his 
previous life; while evil acts hideous in their nature arise from sins. Men after 
having enjoyed the bliss of heaven are by virtue of their religious merits born in 
sacred places. Siu, after having consigned the wicked to hell, impels them later. 
on to birth in a family of ignoble rank. A man can not be saved, so long as 
there is Karma. Savants therefore try to do away :with the Karma. Know- 
ledge, devotion, wisdom, father, mother and son are worth their names if they 
respectively help a person to do away with his Karma. Persons are .liable 
toa dreadful disease which takes the form of enjoyment or suffering according 
to their actions. Then the Vaisnava physician heals that disease with the

52-62. “Indra and even insignificant men can make a gift of their pro- 
porty voluntarily. O saint, if you give mo voluntarily to the king, I may

you weeping like one bewildered? Conjunction and dissociation are brought 
about by time and not by one's discretion. What am I to you, O king, and what 
are you tome? Our mutual rélation has been established by time. Relation 
that subsists between persons only breeds attachment. The mind fondly imagines 


that a certain property is owned by acertain person. So longas the title in a 


thing subsists, sorrow is occasioned by its loss.” So saying that celestial cow

CHAPTER XXV. 


1-10, Náráyapa said, “The king in distress remembered Hari and angrily 
gent a messenger to the Muni." The messenger went to the Muni and said, 
“Listen to the command of my master. He hath said, “O best of Munis, Iam 
your servant, particularly your guest. Either give me the celestial cow or be 
ready to fight with me. Give me your answer after due deliberation.” ‘The 
Muni smiled and gave expression to maxims of truth and morality thus: “Seeing . 
that the king was oppressed by bunger, I brought him to my house and feasted 
him. But he now wants to take away by force Kapila dearer to me than my 
life. Unable to part with her, I shall surely fight with the king.” The messenger 
returned and communicated to the king (who was armed with a shield) the state- 
ment as wade by the Muni. On the other hand, the Muni said to Kapila, 
«What shall I do now? Asa boat is helpless without a pilot, so is the army 
without you.” Hearing this, Kapila gave many weapons to the king, instructed 
bim in military arts and explained to him the use of weapons. She then 
said, *O saint, you will surely gain victory, but you can not be killed, except by 
means of an infallible weapon. But the king is the pupil of Datt&-Treya and 
is an invincible warrior." : 

11-22. Subsequently, the great Muni in command of the vast army went 
out to fight. The king bowed to the saint and then began to fight. Both the 
parties battled very furiously. In the long run, the army of Kapila easily 
conquered the royal army and broke the king’s chariot, bow and shield. The 
king could not conquer Kapila’s army. He was disarmed and fainted. A 
portion of his army perished; the rest ran away. The merciful Muni seeing that 
his royal guest was in a swoon ceased to pursue the enemy. The imaginary 
army of Kapilá dissolved in her body. Then the saint gave the dust of his feet 
to the king, blessed him saying. *' May you be victorivus” ! and restored him to 
consciousness by sprinkling over his body the water of his pot. The king bowed 
low to him with folded hands. The saint embraced and blessed him again, 
bathed and feasted him with care. But the heart of a Brahmin is soft like 
butter; whereas the heart of others is keen like the sharp edge of a razor. It 
could not be easily controlled. The king went to him again and said, ‘O saint, 
give me the cow as desired by me; or, come and fight with me.” á

what do you want to hear ?

by his own acts so was the hermitage of the saint beset by his own arrows. 
As birdsare confined in a cage, so was the royal army subdued or controlled 
by the missile of arrows. The king, seeing that his army was at bay, bowed 
low to the Muni with folded palms along with the auxiliary sovereigns. Blessed 
by the Muni, he delightfully ascended his car. His auxiliaries followed suit. 
The king then aimed at the Muni the sword, the arrow, the mace and the spear. 
The Muni easily cut them asunder and discharged his own weapons which were 
destroyed by the king. 

16-30. The king aimed at the Muni the spear. The latter rendered it 
ineffectual and discharged celestial weapons at the king. The king also in his 
turn destroyed those weapons. He.discharged a spear, but the Muni averted 
itand showered upon the king a missile of arrows. The bodies of the kings ` 
were lacerated by weapons; but as they were held fast by arrows, they could 
not run away, ‘hen the gaping or the yawning weapon which the muni 
discharged rendered insensible the king and other auxiliary sovereigns. The 
Muni finding that the king and all his army (infantry, horse etc) were asleep 
refrained from striking them. He then consoled the weeping Kapil& and was 
about to lead her home when the king suddenly awoke and restrained the Muni 
from his purpose. The frightened cow ran away to her pen; but the Muni re- 
mained there with his bow and arrow with a fearless heart Thereupon the king 
aimed at the Muni the Brahmá-weapon which was baffled by the Brahma weapon 
of the Muni, The Muni with his celestial weapon destroyed the bow, the arrow, 
the car, the charioteer and the amulet of the king. The king thus assailed 
was fled with anger and beheld in his presence the iron-spear named Bka- 
puruga-ná$ini which was given to him by Dattá-Treya. He bowed to Datta- 
Treya, held the weapon which was effulgent like hundreds of suns and revolved 
it in his hands. The gods who arrived there to witness the scene raised a hue 
and cry. Within that weapon, by means of mantra, the king invoked and 
focused the lustre of Brahma, Visnu and Mahesa wherewith he lighted up the 
sky and all the quarters. Then he hurled it with all his might. It assailed 
with a mighty force the heart of the Muni, penetrated it and went to Visnu. 
Vignu gave it, of old, to Datti-Treya who gave it to Karta-Viryya. The Muni 
expired and the lustre of his body passed through the sky to the Brahma-loka.

his death saying, *O my ohild, where art thou gone?" So saying, she went to the 
Goloka and communicated to the Supreme Being, Lord Krisna, seated on & 
diamond throne, the news about the death of Jamadagni. Indays of yore, Visnu 
gave the cow to Brahma, Brahmá gave her to Bhrigu who was pleased to give 
herto Jamadagniat Puskara. When Kapila was passing to the Goloka with 
streaming eyes, the drops Of her tears fell upon the earth and were turned into 
pearls. Then the king Kárta-Viryya restored his army to consciousness, perform- 
ed the penance for the slaughter of a Brahmin and cheerfully went back to his 
capital. The chaste widow of the Muni named Renuka when she heard of his death 
clasped his dead body to her breast and swooned for a while. Onregaining her

genesis to delusion (the imaginative faculty) of God. The names given to things, 
such as jar or picture, are merely symbolic. They are artificial like morning

As the servants follow the king, so do.all the attributes of the body including .

61-67. O child, nobody is the father or the son of any body. This 
relation is a pure fiction. AJl animals, driven by the currents of their acts, 
good or bad, are floating, here and there, on the surface in the sea of the 
world. The wise never weep on account of bereayement in their family. 
My son, being overwhelmed with grief, you should not weep for the death of 
your father. The S'ástras hold that the tears of the scn or the wife bring about 
the degradution of the deceased father or husband. It is mere folly to be—wail 
the loss of one’s relation; for lamentation even lasting for one year cannot bring 
him back to life. When a man’s body is quitted by the Supreme Being, the 
dust of his body returns to the dust, the watery portion goes into the water, the 
sky dissolves into the vast vacuum, the airy portion merges into the wind, the 
fery portion enters into the fire. Tbe deceased person does not listen any 
longer to the lamentations of his relations. When a person dies, the things 
which survive him are the name given to him by his parents, his knowledge, fame 
and good and bad acts: all the rest perish with him. O R&ma, for the benefit of 
your father in the next world, perform his funerals, oblations etc., as prescribed 
by the scriptures. He is really a kinsman or a son who performs the funerals 
of the deceased and contributes to his welfare.” Paragurama when he heard the 
consolatory words of the saint Bhrigu, composed himself and thinking that it 
was useless to mourn desisted from lamentations for the death of his father. 
At that time, the chaste and pious Renuka put questions to the szint Bhrigu.

The most venerable saint Bbrigu replied, “O chaste lady, join your husband.

sorts of rites in relation to her husband. On this day she is entitled to per- 
form any act relative to the gods or the Manes. The last-named act she can also 
do on the fifth day of her menses. As a man who subsists upon snakes forcibly 
drags out a snake out of the hole, so is a virtuons woman able to lift even her 
sinful husband to heaven. A virtuous woman enjoys the company of her 
husband for a period covered by fourteen Indrasand for the same period she sub- 
sequently dwells in Heaven.” So saying, Bhrigu addressed Parasurima thus: “A

at a height of one lakh yojans above the Brahma-loka and is vastly superior

With the speed of mind the great yogi Para$uráma beheld the S'iva-loka. It 
can be compared with nothing else and nothing can be compared with it. He

1-6. Siva then said, “O Brahmin boy, who are you ? where is your home?

of mercy, lord S^iva, I am the son of Jamadagni. Iam descended from the

foolish king inspired with the desire to take possession of the cow killed my 
father in the battle-field; and the sorrowful Kapila went to the Gcloka.

Which deity did he worship with the mantras and what benefits did he derive

an excellent and very sacred hymn which was the outcome of the yoga of 
Vibhüti or the super-human power of the god S'iva. (n the coast of the 
Swayam-prabh& river, in the valley of the diamond-hills, in the forest of the 
Párijáta in a certain hermitage, in the presence of Krisna who is the lord o£ 
the Goloka and the master of the three worlds, S’iva gave him a mantra 
celetrated by the name of the Kalpa-tree which yields all fruits. S/iva said to 
Para$uráma, “O blessed scion of the race of Bhrigu, I love you more than my 
son. Please accept this amulet. O Rama, this is the most valorous of all 
the amulets in the world and is called the conqueror of the three worlds, 
The god to be adored by this amulet is Lord Krisna. Its perusal secures 
victory to the reader in the battle-field. In the sphere of the Rása, in the 
grove of Radha, in the Goloka, Lord Krisna gave it to me. It is sacred and 
excellent. Ilove you very much and hence I give it to you. By wielding 
it, the primordial nature, i.e. the goddess Párvati killed S’umbha, Nisumbha, 
Mahisü-sura and other demons. By wielding it, Ihave become an onto- 
logist and the destroyer of the world and thereby I killed the demon ‘Tripura 
whom it was impossible for others to slay. By wielding it and by its perusal 
Brahma has become the creator and Ananta has been able to bear the 
earth on his head. 


14-24. By wielding it, Kürma sustains Ananta on his back, the wind 
which touches every person and preserves his life has become the refuge of 
the world; Varuna, the god of water, has become an adept, Indra has become 
the king of the gods; theSun by the assumption of a radiant form lights 
up the globe; the moon, the husband of the white water-lily, has become

in the world as the witness of virtue and vice; Saraswati presides over all 
knowledge; Laksmí, the spouse of Hari, takes the form of crops and

the gods; the great saint Sanat Kumára has attained a pre-eminent rank among 
Wise people. Such is the virtue of the amulet which I give to you. You 
may give it to any pious Vaignava disciple of yours. But you should not 
lve it toa traitor or to the disciple of any other Brahmin: foniy on can do 
So only at your peril, i.e. at the risk of your life.” Parasurâma said, O lord,

god; it is meant to secure victory in the three worlds. In heaven, in the 
Mortal world or in the nether world, it is impossible to find an amulet 
Superior to this amulet.

50-58. Whoever baving done Purascharan (repetition of the name of 

a deity with burnt offerings and oblations) with reference to this amulet can 
render it perfect is redeemed in his life-time : such an adept reaps the same 
benefits that can be secured by the worship of Lord Krisna for ten millions 
of years. The virtue of wielding this amulet called the “Conqueror of the three 
worlds” is sixteen times greater than any of the following acts: thousands 
of royal sacrilices, hundreds of Báj-peya sacrifices, countless horse or man- 
sacrifices, the great gift of mountains of crops, ie. heaps of crops, the cireum- 
ambulation round the world with all its seven oceans. The fasting on the 
days of Ohándráyana or Ekádasi, the wielding of the nails or hair, the study 
of the Vedas, Mahá-bhárata $c. the ablution in the holy waters or devotion 
to god is not equal in point of religious efficacy even to a digit of this amulet. 
A man who acquires perfection by mcans of this amulet can easily attain the 
osition of an adept or a god or even the bondage of Hari. Whoever recites 

it ten lakhs oË times becomes an adept and bence omniscient Hvery-thing 
becomes manifest before his eye of wisdom. But whoever worships Krigna 
without understanding the meaning of this amulet can never attain perfection, 
though he may recite it for ten millions of ages. O son Parasuráma, by 
wielding this amulet, you will be able cheerfully and fearlessly to rid the earth 
of the Ksatriyas twenty-one times. Rather leave for good your kizgdom, 
or jurisdiction, get yourself beheaded and thus sacrifice your life than part with 


this amulet even if your lifeis in danger.

59-71, He is the milk among the productions of the cow, the fire among 
sacred articles, the water among sanctifying things. I bow to Him. He is 
the Kaga (the holy grass) among different kinds of grass, the disease among 
enemies, the peace of mind among human qualities. J bow to this wonderful 
Deity. Heis full of light and wisdom. He is the sum-total of all objects. 
He is all pervading and indescribable. I bow to Him. He is the soul of all 
permanent objects. He is omnipresent like the wind. Of all the pervading 
objects, He is the most pervading like the sky: J bow to Him. Who with 
his ordinary intelligence can adore lord Visnu that cannot be described 
even by the four Vedas? Saraswati, the goddess of knowledge, is struck dumb 
in her attempt to eulogise Him. His essence can not be determined by the 
mind or described by language. How isit possible for a savant, then, to adore 
Him? He is like pure light. Out of compassion for His worshippers He 
wields a form, gracious and lovely like a cloud. I bow to the Lord Krisna 
who has two hands and plays on the flute; whois very young; who smiles 
cheerfully; whois always obsorved by the merry wives of the cow-herds at 
Vrindivana. I bow to the Lord whe chews betels offered by Radha andis 
seated on an excellent lovely throne, Heis tended with white chowries by 
His associate cow-herds gorgeously clad; His heart is set upon the amorous 
deeds performed in the Rásá; He occupies the central position in the sphere 
of the Rás& and enjoys the society of the cow-herdesses. I bow to Him. He 
loves to sport at times on the mountain of a hundred summits; and at other 
times, on the diamond-hills, or on the coast of tho Virajf in the Goloka. I 
bow to Him. He is eternal, tranquil, lovely, truthful. He is the consort of 
Radha and identical with Brahma. I bow to Him.

1-8. Nár&yana said, ‘O Narada, Parasuráma bowed to S/iva.and S/iva,

of a glorions light a diamond car eclipsing all the quarters with the brilliance of

2 very fair appearance and smiling face ready to show kindness to His adherents. 
At this sight, Paragurdma fell prostrate on the ground before Him and humbly. 
besought a boon from the god. He ‘said, “O lord of the world, give me a boon 
so that I may be able to rid the earth twenty-one times of the Ksatriyas. May 
I ever remain devoted to your feet and may I acquire the perpetual bondage of 


‚your feet, which can so rarely be secured by any body." Lord Krisna gave 


him the boon solicited and disappeared. Parasur&ma then bowed to the Supreme

to quiver prognosticating good luck.

55-66. The Ksatriyas are the servants of the Brahmins, the Vaisyas

ora Bráhmin. The Brahmins are superior to the gods. If they are pleased,

67-72. Time also preserves in the form of Visnu. Krigna alone has 
been able to conquer time. He alone is the governor of fate, the preserver of 
the preserver and the destroyer of the destroyer. He alone allots destiny to 
mankind. That destiny gives to a devotee the fruits of devotion. No one 
can kill another unless impelled todo so by destiny. By the command of 
Krigna, Brahma creates, Vignu preserves and S'iva destroys the world; the 
winds blow and animate creatures; death swallows the creation and the sun 
rises every day; Indra rains and preserves the crops: the Fire helps a man to 
cook his food by imparting heat; the great Time moves through eternity; the 
earth with her animate and inanimate objects becomes motion-less; the trees 
grow, blossom and fructify at one time and decay at another time.

battle-field is not despicable. Bat it is ridiculous, according to the Vedas and 

social laws, for Brahmins to fight. Inall ages, the maintenance of peace, the 

performance of religious rites with a view to secure welfare and avert calamity 
is the vocation of a Brahmin, Waris not his business. War is the strength 
of the Ksairiyas, trade is the strength of the Vaisyas; the service of the 

Bráhminsis the strength of the S'üdras. Begging is the strength of the men- 

dicants. Hari and faith in Hariconstitute the strength of a Vaisnava. Injury 

is the strength of the wicked: devotion is the strength of the devotee. Toilet 
is the strength of whores; youth is the strength of women; valour is the 
strength of kings; crying is the strength of children; truth is the strength of the 
virtuous; lie is the strength of the vicious; adherence to the leader is the 
strength of followers, accumulation of wealth is the strength of persons 
of moderate means; knowledge isthe strength of the learned; trade is the 
strength of merchants; wealth is the strength of the rich; intellect is the 
strength of pure and tranquil-minded persons; unity is the strength of the 
talented; dishonesty is the strength of thieves; sweet words and hypocrisy 
constitute the strength of pimps; ravenous propensity is the strength of the 
beasts of prey; chastity is the strength of virtuous women; the service of the 
preceptor is the strength of the disciple; virtue is the strength of the house- 
holder; loyalty to the king is the strength of subjects; eulogy is the strength 
of bards; the worship of Brahma is the strength ofthe Brahma-charis; purity 
of manners is the strength of the Yatis; resignation is the strength of the 
recluses (Sannyásis) sin is the strength of sinners; Hari is the strength of the 
helpless; religious sanctity or merit isthe strength of the holy; the king is the 
strength of his subjects; the fruit is the strength of trees. The water is the 
strength of oceans, fish and crops; peace is thestrength of kings, particularly 
the Brahmins. I have ;never seen or heard that a Brahmin is impatient for 
war. Alas! in the regime of Hari, even, matters have been thus perverted,”

1-13. On the demise of Suchandra his son Puskaráksa, effulgent like

armies under bis command. He wielded round his neck the amulet of the 
great goddess of fortune by virtue of which he gained prosperity and conquered 
the three worlds. The wise brothers of Para$uráma came to the battle-field.

arrows. The Brahmin heroes cut off the chariot of the king with five arrows, 
his bow with seven arrows, his chrioteer with five arrows and the horses of the 
chariot with ten arrows. They also cut the quiver of the king with seven 
arrows, and killed his brothers with the spear of S'iva. The heroic saints 
destroyed three of the regiments of the king and hurled at him S'iva's spear 
for bis destruction. ‘That spear adorned the king's neck like a garland of 
lotus. Then the Br&hmins were highly incensed and hurled at him mace,

came into contact with the body of theking. The brothers of Paraguréma

not give this amulet to ordinary people. You should give it to an obedient -

adores Maha-Laksmi, (the mother of the universe,) will not attain accomplish-

81-104. Râma fell flat on the ground. They all took him in the lap,

he begets and supports the issue. But he who givesfood for the maintenance 
of the child is superior to the father, as without food, itis impossible for a 
man to live. The origin from father is only natural. But the mother who 
wields the childin her womb and supports him is a hundred times superior 
to both the kinds of father as specified above, The Vedas hold that the 
totelary deity is far superior to the above. But the religious preceptor excels 
all or any of tha above-named. The son of a preceptor is adorable like the 
preceptor; but the wife of the preceptor is more adorable. A preceptor 
preserves his disciple from the wrath of thetutelary deity. But if the gurü 
be displeased, no one’ can save the disciple. It therefore follows that the 
gurü is Brahma, Visnu, Mahesa or even the great Brahma. Hoe is dearer 
than the Brahmins. It is he who inspires in the mind faith for Hari. Who 
then, can bea greater friend than the religious preceptor ? Who, indeed, 
is a greater friend than the preceptor, when he points out the right path 
to people (benighted by the darkness of ignorance) with the burning lamp 
of knowledge in his hand? Who isa greater friend than the gurü by the 
recitation of whose mantra a man obtains omniscience and acquires all sorts of 
perfection ? By the dint of the knowledge obtained from him, a person easily 
gains victory in every place. No oneis a greater friend than the guru who 
imparts knowledge for which heis adored in the whole world. If actuated 
by the conceit of learning or owing to kinship, a man fails to revere his gurü, 
he is, unquestionsbly, implicated in a sin greater than the Bráhmin-slaughter. 
Whoever, (seeing that his gurü is poor or insignificant or fallen) hehayes 
with him as if he were an ordinary mortal is rever consecrated (though he 
may batho in holy streams) and is not privileged to do any act. © son, Lord 
Krigna is your tutelary deity and Siva is your preceptor. You should therefore 
seok the protection of the latter who has holped you to rid the earth twenty- 
one times of the Ksatriyas and who has inspired in your mind faith Pr pos

this goddess. ^O saint, in days of yore, when the Bods were attacked by the 
demonsin war, this goddess manifested herself from the valour of the gods;

gods their position and was subsequently born as the daughter of Daksa as the

23-34. Narayana said, “O consecretor of your family, truly are you

You have been redeemed in your life-time; you are a saint devoted to Hari, You

this is why you desire to attend to the narratives of Hari. The place where 
Haris account is recited is visited by geds, Munis and all the holy 
shrines of the world. ‘The saints, by attending to the recitations of . Hari's 
account, ultimately attain a position free from all danger: and the place 
where it is recited is turned into a shrine orboly land. He who recites 
it not only sancties the audience but hundreds of his own ancestors, 
One who queries on subjects relating to Hari at once consecrates one's geneology. 
The audience likewise consecrate their own families and friends. A man who 
drinks the nectar of the name of Hari consecrates his birth in India which he 
has been able to assume by a process of endless devotions. ‘There are nine 
indications of the faith in Hari. They are as follows: adoration of Hari, his 
worship, the recitation of his mantras, his service, his recollection, recitation of 
his hymns, listening to a description of his attributes, surrender of self to Hari 
and his bondage. 


35-47. A man devoted to Hari is free from danger and becomes longevous.

him. As the grasshoppers do not goto the fire, so the myrmidons of Yama 
do not approach him even in dream. He is not visited by disease, danger, grief 
or any sort of obstacle; death cannot touch him throngh fear. Ihe gods, 
saints and adepts are ali pleased with him. By the grace of Hari he is treo 
from fear and «emains ever happy. It is in the fitness of things that you 
are anxious to listen to Hari's narratives. This causes no surprise; for you 
have sprung out of the mind of Brahma, A man's mind is moulded according 
to his origin. Your father Brahmá has become the creator by ministering 
to the lotus-feet of Hari. He who cherishes for Hari the nine kinds of faith 
as specified above, who is fond of the narratives of Hari, whose mind is immersed

children as the creatures of Hari. He who is kind to all creatures and regards

ihe sight o£ the sun. A sinner is redeemed by the touch of a Vaisnava; and

1-14. “O Narada, when Rádhà did not find Hari in that mansion of

than your former self. O chaste one, your former self has been turned into 
ariver. Now assume a new shipe, get up from the river and approach me.” 
Thereupon, the smiling Viraja, in yellow dress, beautiful like Radha, came out of 
the river and cast a glance at Hari, the lord of her life. She was bending 
with the weight of her buttocks and loins. She hada rising breast. Her 
gait was that of an elephant. She was the most honourable of the honorable 
nymphs, and the fairest of the fair. ‘The grace of her body was like the hue 
of Champaka; her lips wore like bimbas: her teeth were like grains of pome 
granate ; her face was like the full moon; har eyes were like blooming lotuses ; 
her forehead was marked with vermilion and musk. She was decorated with 
graceful leaves and her head was decked with a charming chignon. The 
ornament constructed with the pearl produced in the head of the elephant was 
suspended on thefront of her nose. Her neck was adored with a garland of 
pearls. Her arms were decked with armlet, brace-let and S’ankha (ornament 
made of shell for the wrists of women). She was also adorned with the tink- 
ling girdle of small bells and anklets. ‘he lord of the universe at the sight

gods having thus worshipped Lord Krişna who fulfils the desires of his votaries 
stood up before him with a gratified heart. Hari, the asylum of mercy, smiled 
with approbation and used to them words which were beneficial and truthful. 
He said, “O gods, just come to my house and rest a while. When you have 
sought my protection, it behoves me to enquire iuto your welfare. 


27-41. Set your heart at rest. You need not be anxious when I am here, 
I exist in a latent condition in all things; but I become manifest when I am 
adored. I have known all your purpose. O gods, the good and the evil are 
the outcome of time. Time determines big and small acts. Trees in time 
are loaded with raw or ripe fruits. Pleasure and pain, prosperity and calamity, 
grief and anxiety, good and evil deeds are occasioned by time, as the result of 
Karma. In the three worlds, no one is lovely or hateful ; but every one becomes 
so in process of time. Impelled by Karma, the kings aud manus are rendered 
subservient to time. Nay, the time that has just been passed in the Goloka 
is equivalent to seven Manwantaras of the earth; and in this interval, seven Indras 
have already disappeared. The jurisdiction of the eighth Indra has set in. 
Indra, Manu and the kings are all subject to time ; only their fame, land and 
infallible virtue survive them in name. The wheel of my time in this way 
constantly revolves. At present, the kings of the world are wicked, blasphemous 
and very powerful. But they shall submit to the yoke of time in due course. 
That time has now come. The wind blows constantly, the fire consumes, the 
sun gives heat, death haunts animals, and the cloud showers rain at my com- 
mand. Enjoined by me and through my fear the Br&hmins are engaged in 
devotion, the saints are devoted to the worship of Brahma and the Yogis are 
absorbed in meditation. But my votaries destroy Karma by the root and are 
hence fearless. 


49-52. gods, I am the time of time, the destiny of destiny, the destro- 
yer of the destroyer, the preserver of the preserver ana the Lord Supreme. 
Hara is called the destroyer because he destroys by my command. You, by: 
virtue of the function allotted to you, are called the creator. Dharma is so; 
called because of his preservation of virtue. lam the Lord of every thing 
from Brahma down to a bush. I give fruits according to Karma and eradicate 
them likewise. None cin preserve those whom I must destroy. None can 
destroy those whom I -must preserve. I am the destroyer, the preserver and 
creator of all. Put I can not destroy my worshippers who always wield forms. 
My adherents are always submissive to me and adore my feet. I always dwell 
by their side for their preservation. In this world a creature is born and des- 
troyed, again and again. But my worshippers are immortal, fearless and free 
from allanxieties, ‘This is why savants want to render me homage. Such men: 
are truly blessed. ‘The rest are deluded. Every one engaged in Karma has to 
Suffer torments occasioned by birth, death, decrepitude, disease and fear.. 
But these evils can, by no means, affect my worshippers. My worshippers are 
never implicated in acts of virtue or vice. I am, as it were, the life of my 
worshippers; and the worshippers also constitute my life. I always remember 
those who remember me. ~

Every one looked at the most enchantiog form of Vignu. He wielded earrings 
and a diadem. He was clad in yellow dress : he was dark-blue like a new cloud 
and was in the prime of his youth. . His body was besmeared with sandal,

like the lotus. He was seated on a throne of gem, attended by his retinue and 
adored by the gods. S’iva worshipped and eulogised him. Parvati at his sight 
veiled her face and was much abashed. But she continued to look at him with 
eyes wide open, glanced at his wonderful dress and was filled with raptures. 


154-146. At one time she saw S'iva of golden hue, haying five faces and 
three eyes, armed with an axe and trident and fair as ten millions of Cupids. 
At other times she saw the dark-blue, four-handed Visnu in yellow dress hay- 
ing two eyes and one face only. She then concluded that both the forms are 
the modifications of the same indivisible Brahma. But being bewildered by 
the Maya or illusion of Visnu and excited by Just, she cogitated thus: “ S’iva, 
Brahma and Visnu are all the outcome of my digits. But of these three,” 
Visnu is vested with the attribute of virtue. Hence he is the best of the three.” 
A thrill of raptures agitated her frame and she worshipped the Supreme Being. 
Then the omniscient Lord S’ankara knew what was passing in her mind and 
addressed her, using truthful and beneficial words thus, * O daughter of the 
mountain, I request you to embrace the supreme Lord Hari. Brahma, Visnu 
and myself are not separate from the Supreme Brahma. But owing to change 
of circumstances, we have taken different forms. You, as Nature, are unique. 
You can take any form and are the mother of all. Youare the Vani of 
Brahma, the Laksmt residing in the heart of Narayana and Durga ruling my 
heart. O chaste one, you ought to know this psychic mystery.

Ananta sustains Vasudhá, the tortoise sustains Ananta, the wind sustains the 
tortoise. I sustain the wind, and you sustain myself. [t follows, then, that 
I always reside in you. You are the symbol of all energy and the primordial 
nature. You represent the body and harbour the three attributes, I am your 
soul, free from all desire, but your association vests me with will-power. The 
semen emanates from the male and produces offsprings, but the woman, who 
is the outcome of the digits of Nature, is the receptacle of that semen. 


212-224. The body and the soul cannot reside apart from one another. 
Thus the superiority of both is equally established. Without the co-operation 
of both, creation is impossible. © Radha, why do you entreat me in vain ? We 
both constitute the germ of the world. There is no difference between you and 
me. ‘The soul remains where the body remains. Both of them are identical. 
As whiteness is inseparable from milk, heat from the fire, smell from the earth, 
coldness from the water, so I am inseparable from you. There is not the 
slightest chance of our separation. O beauty, as a potter cannot build a pot with- 
out clay, as a goldsmith can not construct a jewel without gold, so am I Incap- 
able of creation without you. Without me, you are life-less, and without you, 
Iam invisible, As the soul is eternal, so you who are all powerfal and the con- 
tainer of the universe are also eternal. fakgmi, the auspicious Saraswati, 
Brahmá, S'iva, Ananta, Dharma, &o., are dear to me as my life. But you are 
dearer to me than my life. Had it not been so, you would not have occupied 
my heart and the gods and goddesses would not have sat by my side. Radha,

45-53. When the womb was filled with wind, Lord Krisna who i 
never associated with matter, presided over the lotus of Daivaki’s heart, Thus she 
passed ber daysin very great pain. By turns she stood up, sat down, walked 
for a while and slept. The high-minded Vasu-Deva perceived that the time of 
delivery was approaching. He then remembered Hari. Agitated with fear, he 
introduced into the house (presided over by Daiyaki) sword, iron, fire, 
people versed in the mantras, the wives of his friends or relations, his kith and 
kin and the Brahmins. When two prabars of the night were passed, the sky 
was over-cast, lightning flashed and seven kinds of wind began to blow. The 
guards were benumbed with sleep and Jay like dead men on their beds. In the 
meantime the gods assembled there; and Brahma, S%iva and Dharma addressed 
the Lord in the womb. The gods said, ** Lord, you are the cause of the world; 
you do not emanate from the womb of any woman; you have no end; you 
are inexhaustible. You are full of light and glory aud free from sins. You 
are great. You have attributes and yet you are free fromattributes. You 
are uncontrolled and formless, but you take a form out of deference to your 
worshippers- You can do anything according to your will. You are the lord 
and prop of all. You are the source of pleasure and pain. You are inaccessible 
to all and destroy the wicked. You are indivisible and fearless. You are the 
receptacle of all things in the world. No calamity can subdue you. You are 
free from title or attributes, unassociated with anything, exempt from desire. 
You are the death of death. You are the eternal spirit, contented or absolutely 
satisfied, spotless, eternal, blessed (or very fortunate), Durbhaga, i. e., unfortu- 
nate or not possessed of good fortune, eloquent and indestructible and you 
can be adored with difficulty. You are the cause, the form and the auxiliary 
branch of the Vedas. You are God.’ With this hymn, the gods bowed 
to him, again and again; and with eyes streaming with raptures, they rained 
flowers on that sacred place. Whosver at break of day reads the above hymn 
consisting of forty-two names of the Lord acquires firm faith in Hari and 

secures the bondage of Hari, as desired by him. 


54-67. The gods -went home after having pronounced the above eulogy. 
All life seemed to be extinct at Mathurá ; and the land was flooded 
with rain-water. The night was very dark. When the seventh Muhürta. (One 
Muhürta is equal to forty-eight minutes) was passed and the eighth Muháürta 
arrived, in the midnight, atan auspicious moment marked by propitious Stars, 
shunned by evil stars and characterised by the predominance ot Rohint (the 
fourth lunar mansion), the eighth day of the lunar month, Jayanti— Yoga and 
the rise of the half-moon, the sun and the good and bad planets, feeling the 
approach of Lord Hari who isincomprehensible and outside the scope of the 
Vedas, transgressed their proper order and fixed themselves timidly at the sign 


of Pisces in the zodiac. All the planets were propitiated andat the instance 
ot Brahma gladly occupied the eleventh place in the zodiac. There was a 
shower of rains from the sky ; and the earth with allher quarters assumed a 
cheerful aspect,

merrily, fire was kindled up: the sound of the drum and the trumpet filled the

At that time Daivaki dropped on the ground and at once all the pent-up wind 
emanated from her womb. At that time Lord Krisna wielded a celestial 
form and emanated from the heart of Daivaki, His face was lovely and 
enchanting. Hehad two hands holding a flute. Earrings of the cut of a fish 
decorated his features. His face was smiling and cheerful. He was deoked 
with gems and anxious to favour his votaries. He was clad in yellow 
and besmeared with musk, saffron and sandal paste. He was dark-blue like a 
new cloud. His face resembled the autumnal full moon. His lips were like 
the bimbas. His head was covered with the plumage of a peacock and a 
diadem constructed with pure gems. His waist was crooked. He was covered 
with wreaths of wild flowers. His breast was adorned with a whorl of hair and 
the gem called Kaustubha. Ho had three-fold curvatures in his body. He | 
was young in age, tranquil in disposition and looked more lovely than Brahma 

or S/iva. Both Vasudeva and Daivaki were very much astonished at the sight 
and with folded palms and streaming eyes, they reverentially adored him with 
the following hymn.

102-116. So saying, Hari wielded the form of a boy. Vasudeva was spell-

was looking at the roof of the house. He was much astonished to see her, took 
her in his lap, placed the boy there and came io Mathura in great mental 
agitation. ‘Then he came to the room of his lying-in spouse and placed there 
the weeping child who represented the grand principle of illusion (Maha-Maya), 
Daivaki was much pleased to see the.girl. The sleeping guards were awakened 
by her cries. They took her to Kamsa. Daivaki and Vasudeva followed them 
in grief, The sight of the girl did not give much pleasure to Kamsa; he had 
not the slightest compassion for that weeping child. The cruel Kamsa was 
about to hurl her ona piece of stone with a view to kill her. At this sight 
Vasudeva and Daivaki modestly thus said to Kamsa: “O best of kings, you 
are a great politician ; therefore deign to hear my words full of truth and 
morality. O foremost of my relations, you have killed six of our sons. Haye 
yOu no compassion? How will the destruction of the eighth issue of Aum 
mere daughter, redound to your credit? She can never kill you in battle- 

ield. :

1-19. “O great saint,” said Narada again, * kindly tell me in brief the 
fruits of the vow of Janmástami the ceremony of Jayanii-Yoga and the method 
whereby the vow is performed. What is the nature of the delinquency if the 
vow isnot observed and if people eat on that day ? On the other hand, what 
benefit does a person reap if he observes that vow, if he keeps fast and takes 
the first meal after the fast?” Náráyana said, “ In the first place, on the seventh 
lunar day, a person witha pure heart and clean body should take only boiled 
rice and ghee. While taking ihe first meal after fast, he should adopt the 
same course. On the eight lunar day, he should get up from bed at dawn, 
perform his morning rites and after bathing, he should make an avowal of 
the purpose for which the vow is observed. Abstinence observed in the course 
of a vow is only meant toatford satisfaction to Hari. The fruit which a 
person reaps by bathing and worshipping on the eighth lunar day in the month 
of Bhadra is ten millions of times as great as the fruit which he secures by 
ablutions and the worship of Hari lasting for a Manwantara. Whoever on this 
day offers even a drop of water to the manes reaps benefits equal to the benefit 
which he could secure by the performance of the funeral ceremony of his 
ancestors at Gay& for one-hundred years. On this day, the worshipper having 
bathed and performed the necessary or ordinary diurnal rites ought to construct 
a house of confinement for child-birth, place there iron, sword andifre and engage 
guards, He should also keep there a pair of scissors for cutting the navel-string 
of a new-born child and secure the services of a mid-wife there, Then he

reward is secured by fasting and keeping up the night on the occasion? 
What is the time fixed for the observance of the vow ? What sin is incurred 
by eating on that day? Kindiy tell me all this, after due reference to the 
auxiliary branches of the Vedas and the code of tha Puranas.” Nárüyna 
said, “That day when in the midnight even a quarter of Astamt (the eighth 
day of ths lunar month) subsists is tho most celebrated occasion; and on that 
day Hari was born. ‘his conjunction of the times is calculated to give religious 
sanctity and victory. Hence it is called Jayanti-Yoga. Savants should 
fast, perform the vow and keep up tho night on this occasion. This time is 
pre-eminently suited to the vow and sanctioned by the Vedántists and Brahma. 


Whoever keeps up the night and fasts in the way specified above is redeemed . 


from the sins accumulated by him in ten millions of births. He who performs 
the vow should carefully leave out or exclude the Astimi which is combined 
with the Saptami (the seventh day of tho lunar month). Such an Astamt 
even if it be conjoined with Rohini should bo avoided 
during the predominance of the mansion of Rohini 
combined with Saptami. When this Rohini which is jealously conserved in 
the Vedasandin the commentary of the V»das and is particularly auspicious 
passes away, the worshipper should take his first meal aftor the fast, after having 
remembered Hari and the gods. his breakfast is most sacred and calculated 
to redeem the ancestors of the worshipper and give him reward and sancti y

recur in a century. He who observes the vow on this day cedeems ten millions 
of his ancestors thereby. Krisna is pleased even with those adherents of his 
who simply fast on that day bat cannot observe the vow through poverty, 
A man initiated in the Jayanti vow and keeping up the night with various 
ingredients sacred to the vow gets abundance of reward from the Lord, the 
enemy of the demons. Aman who does not misrepresent his wealth d e. 
who does not pretend to be poor in spiteof his wealth) gets ample reward. 
But a man making such idle pleas gets no reward. A savant should not 
break.his fast on the eighth day of the lanar month or during the predom- 
mance of the fourth lunar mansion, otherwise he will forfeit the religious 
sanctity previously acquired by him and also the fruits of his abstinence. The 
above lunar day destroys fruits eight times and the above mansion, four times, 
Therefore the observer of the vow should break his fast when the above lunar 
day and mansion are passed. If, however, they expire at mid-night, then he 
Should not break his fast at that time but on the third day. As soon as six 
Muhûrtas of the night pass away, midnight sets in: and eating at this time 
is as sinful as the murder ofa Brahmin. Let alone other food, even betel, 
fruit, water, &c., are prohibited in the midnight like beef, ordure and urine, 
The night consists of three watches leaving out four dandas of the day (a danda 

is equal to twenty-four minutes! in the beginning and ihe same period in the 
end. The portion thus left out is called Sandhya (evening or morning) 

A person who is awake and observes his vow on the pure day of Janmástamj 

is relieved from sins of a hundred births, A person who on that day does 
not keep up the night but merely fasts still gets the fruits secured by the 
ceremony of horse-sacrifice. A wretch who eats on that day practically ravishes 


his mother and is guilty of the sin of a hundred Brabmin-slaughters. The 


religious merits acquired by him in ten millions of births are lost by that act 
and he is not entitled to perform any religious rites relating to the gods or tha 
manes. He becomes impure and ultimately dwells in the hell called the thread ’ 
of time (being devoured by worms whose teeth are as sharp as spears) as long 
as the sun and the moon exist. Then he is born in India; but for sixty- 
thousand years he.has to range as a worm in the ordure. Afterwards fora 
thousand crores of years, he is born as a vulture; a hundred times as a boar,. 

a hoast of prev and a jackal and seven times as a snake and crow. Then he 
is born as a man afflicted with 'eprosy and physical pain. Next he. is born as 

a enake-catcher and afterwards as a wicked robber who kills people. Then 
he is born as a washerman, then as an oil-man, then as an impure Devala - 
Brahmin, I£ any one is unable to fast, he ought to feast at least one Brahmin 
or give bim wealth double the quantity of the food Or he ought to mutter 
the mantras of Savitri a thousand times or practise Pranáyám (stoppage of 
Tespiration) as an accessory to meditation, twelve times. I thus narrated - 
to you the subject relating to fasting and the method of the observances of the 
vow, as I heard from Dharma.

the promise he had held out to Radha before(?) What is the form of tho sphere

the sports of the Lord in the sphere of the Rasa and in the water of Yamuna, 
Lord, what was the nature of the devotion performed oy Rohini and Yasoda ? | 
Where was Baladeva born before Hari appeared? The wonderful account of Hari 
is as delightful as nectar ; and particularly if it comes from the mouth of a 
poet, it acquires a new aspect at every step. The descriptio. of ascene witnessed 
by the author with his own eyes is superior to a second-hand description of the 
same. You are a digit of Lord Krisna and the head of the preceptors of the 
gurus of the meditative ascetics. He from whose digits a person has come out 
thrills that person with the joy which he himself feels. You just said to 
mo that both of you brotbers had merged in the lotus feet of Hari. Therefore 
you are an outcome of the digits of the Lord of the Goloka and as magnani- 
mous as he.” Narayana said, “Brahma, S'iva, Avanta Ganesa, Dharma, Kürma, 
myself and Kartikeya are the produets of the digits of Krisna. Who can 
describe the glory of the lord of the Goloka? When we could not compre- 
hend him, how could savants do so? Varaha, Bamana, Kalki, Buddha, 
Mina have emanated from Hari’s digits. Besides these, there are countless 
incarnations, the product of time. Rama and Nrisimha are perfect incarna- 
tions of the deity and reside in the white islands; whereas in the Vaikuntha 
and Goloka, Lord Krisna is the Supreme Being Himself. 

14-21. AtGokula and inthe Goloka he is vested with two hands holding a 
flute and is the consort of Radha; whereas in the Vaikuntha he is vested with 
four hands and isthe lord of Kamala. The Yogis constantly contemplate 
his abstract light; but the Vaignavas adore his lotus feet. How is that Jight 
possible, unless there bea person who possesses that light? Now I tell you by 
what kind of devotions, Yosodà, Nanda and Rohini were able to behold the lotus 
feet of Hari. In the period gone by, Nanda was ihe best of the Vasus and a great 
ascetic named Drona. Dhara was his wife. That Dharà was born as Yasod&; and: 
Kadru, the mother of the snakes, was born as Rohini. Now listen to the descrip- 
tion of their births. Once upon atime, Dhar& and Drona went close to the 
hermitage of Gautama in the Gandha-mádan mountain in the sacred land of 
India and there on the coast of the Suprabha, they prayed for ten thousand. 
years in order to obtain a glimpse of Lord Krisna. After ihe Japse of that period 
when the Lord did not turn up they constructed a pit of fire and, led by a 
RM of stoic apathy to the world, were about to consign themselves to the 

ame. 


22-30. When they were about to commit suicide there was a voice

Where was Baladeva born before Hari appeared? The wonderful acoount of Hari 
is as delightful as nectar; and particalarly if it comes from the mouth, of a 
poet, it acquires a new aspect at every step. The descriptio. of a scene witnessed 
by the author with his own eyes is superior to a second-hand description of the 
same. You are a digit of Lord Krisna and the head of the preceptors of the 
gurus of the meditative ascetics. He from whose digits a person has come out 
thrills that person with the joy which he himself feels. You just said to 
me that both of you brothers had merged in the lotus feet of Hari. Therefore 
you are an outcome of the digits of the Lord of the Goloka and as magnani- 
mous as he.” Narayana said, ‘Brahma, S/iva, Anania Ganesa, Dharma, Kürma, 
myself and Kartikeya are the products of the digits of Krisna. Who can 
describe the glory of the lord of the Goloka? When we could not compre- 
hend him, how could savants do so? Vardha, Bamana, Kalki, Buddha, 
Mina have emanated from Haris digits. Besides these, there are countless 
incarnations, the product of time. Rama and Nrisimha are perfect incarna- 
tions of the deity and reside in the white islands; whereas in the Vaikuntha | 
and Goloka, Lord Krisna is the Supreme Being Himself. | 


14-21. At Gokula and in the Goloka he is vested with two hands holding a 
flute and is the consort of Radha; whereas in the Vaikuntha ho is vested with 
four hands and is the lord of Kamala. The Yogís constantly contemplate ; 
his abstract light; but the Vaisnavas adore his lotus feet. How is that Jight 
possible, unless there bea person who possesses that light? Now I tell you by 
what kind of devotions, Yosodà, Nanda and Rohini were able to behold the lotus 
feet of Hari. ln the period gone by, Nanda was the best of the Vasus and a great

tion of their births. Once upon a time, Dhara and Drona went close to the 
hermitage of Gautama in the Gandha-mádan mountain in the sacred land of 
India and there on the coast of the Suprabhá, they prayed for ten thousand. 
years in order to obtain a glimpse of Lord Krisna. After ihe lapse of that period 
when the Lord did not. turn up they constructed a pit of fire and, led by & 
pane of stoic apathy to the world, were about to consign themselves to the

1-17. Nár&yüna said, “Once upcn a time the chaste Yasodà with the

Then the demon in the guise of a wind seized Lord Hari, whirled him 
round and round and took him at a distance of a hundred Yojans. 
Many branches of the trees were broken. Gokula was filled with darkness. 
Then the wily demon came back and dropped down there with a mighty crash. 
As the demon came in contact with the body of Hari, his sufferings were over 
(4. e, he died) and he ascended a beautiful car and went to the region of Hari. 
In days of yore, this demon was the king of the Pandya regions. Through 
the curse of Durvásá, he had acquired the form of a demon, but was redeemed 
by the touch of the lotus feet of Hari. Everyone at Gokul was bewildered by 
the power of the wind ; and the cowherds and cowherdesses not finding the

Brahmin recited a certain amulet which [ am just going to describe, When 
Hari lay in water in days of antiquity, he gave this amulet to Brahma 
when the latter while roaming in his navel and afraid of the demons 
named Madhu and Kaitava sought his protection. Yoga-Nidra (the goddess 
of mystic devotion and sleep) said thus to Brahma, “ Lord, be of good cheer. 
You need not fear while Hari and I are ready to protect you. May Hari preserve 
your face. May the enemy of Madhu protect your head. May Lord Krişna 
preserve your eyes; may the lord of RAdh& Yours preserve nose. May Mádhava 
preserve your ears and forehead. May Govinda preserve your cheek. May 
KKesava preserve your hair, May Hrisi-kesa preserve your lips and chins, 
May Gadágraja preserve your teeth; ltáse$wara, your tongue, Bamana, your

Janfirdana, your navel; Visnu, your jaws; Purusottama, your buttocks and 
private parts ; the lord of Jànki, your knees: Nrisimha, your arms; Varáüha, 
your feet. May Narayana preserve you above and Kamalapati, below. May 
Gopála preserve you in the east; may the destroyer of Hávana preserve you 
in the south-east corner; Vaikuntha, in the south-west corner, Vanamáli in the 
south, Vasudeva in the west; Aja Vistar-srav4, in the north-west corner; Ananta 
in the north; God, in the north-east corner ; and the victor c£ Indra, in all places. 
May Raghava preserve you in the sky and while you are asleep. This is the most 
wonderful amulet that I have narrated to you. In days of yore, while there 
was a dreadful tight with Sumbha, a fight which dazzled the eye-sight, Lord 
Krisna kindly gave it to meas soon as I remembered him. As I got this 
amulet, Hari at once came to the sky and prepared to devise means for my 
rescue. In days gone by, I fought a dreadful battle for a century with the 
demon called Sumbha and obtained this amulet by virtue of which I killed 
him. After the death of Sumbha the merciful Govinda gave to me in the sky 
the amulet and a wreath of flowers and went to the Goloka. Through the 
influence of this amulet, O godly saint, you need not fear: particularly when 
we are ready to support you,

1-14. Once upon a time, Yasodá went to bathe in the Yamuná. 
- Krisna saw that the house was full of condensed milk, cream, butter, etc. He

came parched; she grew more and more angry. Then the merciful Lord Krigna,

» 1-12. Once upon a time, Nanda went to Vrindávana with Krigna and near

dissociated from you, people call me merely IXrisna. But associated with you,

15-85. Narada said, “O lord, by your grace, I heard wonderful stories.

and good graces of the husband; its presiding deities are hádhá and Krişna. 
It begins on the occasion of Visnu-Sankranti ( the passage of the sun from one 
sign to the next, hence the last day of the month) and ends on Daksináyana 
(the sun's motion towards the south of the equator). Oa the day preceding 
the vow, one should be abstemious by taking only ghee and boiled rice. The 
person observing the vow should bathe on the last day of the mouth of 
Vaisükha and make anavowal of the purpose for which she makes the vow - 
on the coast of the Ganges She should then worship either on a jar cons- 
trueted with gems or on the Holy stone or on the water. At first she should 
worship the five gods and then meditate the Lord of ltàdhá reverentially.

132 143. The person in whose house you live becomes prosperous. The 
person whose house you shun ought to die rather than live. Brahma, Visnu 
and I are symbo!s of energy. By your grace we have ‘een vested with energy 
and thus create, preserve and destroy. Who are Himalaya, I, Kartika and 
Ganega ? Without you, we are powerless; and with you, we become gods. 
The S'rutis hold that it is proper for a virtuous woman to carry out the 
commands of her husband. Therefore, it seems, you await my orders to per- 
form the vow. Very well; but youshould perform it ona grander scale than 
others have done. Lord Sanatakumára will be your priest. I shall provide you 
with Brahmins, lotuses and ingredients. Fair one, appoint Kuvera as your 
treasurer. and myself as the superintendent of gifts. Laksmi will distribute 
gifts. The god of fire will be the cook; the Yaksas will be bearers of 
articles; Kartika will superintend the above articles; the god of wind will 
soavenge all places; Indra will dispense food; and the moon-god will be the 
presiding officer. The sun will decide who are the fit persons to whom gifts 
should be made. O beauty, after having duly presented the articles prescribed 
for the vow, a larger number of fruits and flowers should be offered to Hari. 
Goddess, feast as many Brahmins as have been fixed for the purpose of this 
vow; but invite countless Rrihmins. Afterwards on the seventh day distribute 

to them gold, corals and gems together with sacerdotal fees." 

Thus the vow was initiated and Parvati was more particular 
in her observances on this occasion. The Brahmins could not bear the load 
of their gifts owing to their super-abundance. Thus, O Narada, you have heard

When Brahma saw that the chaste Kalávati was frightened and weeping at the 
sight of the ead condition of her husband, his mind was filled with compassion. 
He appeared bef:re her and himself wept. Then he sprinkled the water of the 
wooden pot in his hand over the body of the king and revived him by means 
of the faculty which he acquired through his supreme knowledge of Brahma.

virtuous woman. Devotion to her husband is her only vow and religion. Of

the thread of time so long as the Sun and the Moon exist where they are bitten

Even virtue is not greater, noris wealth more precioas to her than her 
husband. ln fact, the husband is unsurpassed. The mind of a chasto woman 
is set upon her husband in the same manner as the mind of a Vaisnava is set 
upon the lotus feet of Hari, as the mother's heart upon her only son, as the 
miser’s mind upon his accumulated gold, as the mind of a timid person 
upon peril, as the mind of a learned man upon science, as the heart of 
a suckling upon the breast which it sucks, as the mind of an architect 
upon artistic work and as the mind of a prostitute upon her gallant. Death is 
welcome to a good woman who lives without her husband, Life to such a woman 
is more painful than death. 


97-106. Sorrow is relieved in course of time by eating and drinking, 
But the case is reverse with a woman afflicted with the sorrow caused by 
separation from her husband. For such sorrow is increased by eating and 
drinking. Karma, (causation), shadow and a chaste woman are the constant 
companions of a man. Of these, a chaste wife is the chief. Hyven after 
the body, the seat of enjoyment and suffering, is extinct, a chaste wife is 
the constant companion of a person at every birth. O ruler of the world, if you 
emancipate my husband without me, I will involve you in the sin of the murder 
of an inrocent woman." Brahma was astonished and frightened at her words 
and gently replied thus, “Child, I will not emancipate your husband without 
you ;but at present I cannot emancipate you both. O mother, it is a fact 
recognised by all that the emancipation of a porson is impossible unless he or she 
reaps the consequences of his or her act in the shape of enjoyment or suffering, 
When the sufferings and enjoyments of a person are over, he or she is then 
only entitled to emancipation. O chaste lady, you should under the circums- 
tances enjoy the bliss of heaven along with your husband for sometime. 
After a while, both of you will be born in India : and Rádhá will, in due course, 
be born as your dauguter in that place. After Hádhá's birth, both of you 
willbe redeemed in your life-time and go tothe Goloka. O best of kings, 
you had better enjoy the society of your wife for sometime. Saints are 
vested with the principle of virtue ; therefore it is impossible for you to curse 
me. Saints whose hoart is always set upon the lotus feet of Hari and who 


are impartial to all creatures always court the precious bondage of Hari They 
do not desire emancipation.”

Now I am going to narrate to you the 


main point in my acoount which destroys sins as fire consumes fuels. Viswa- 
karma, the best of engineers, after having constructed the mansion of Vrişa- 


bhánu went away with his attendants to some other place, This architect (who 
knows ontology) after due deliberation constructed there the lovely hermitage 
of Nanda only two miles in expanse. With great discrimination, he constructed 
the house which vastly excelled others. Hound it, he excavated four moats 
which were fortified with stones and could not possibly be surmounted by 
enemies, Near the moat, he set up many gardens where the wind redolent of 
the Champak and other flowers gladdened all the sides. Hundreds of trees 
of mangos, betels, jacks, dates, pomegranates, baels, oranges, limes, Bhringas 
(poisonous plants), hog-plums and black-berries came into existence. Screw- 
pine, kadam and others trees loaded with fruits and flowers surrounded the 
moat and added to its beauty. The solitudes of that place were fit for amorous 
pastimes and desirable in every respect. 


157-171. He also constructed a passage for communication in one of 
ihe secret places of the moat. It was so designed as to be impregnable to the 
enemies and to afford an easy access to the friends. For, across that passage, 
he constructed a pillar which was merged in a small-quantity of water. The 
Space covered by the pillar was neither very long nor broad, Above the 
moat, ho constructed a rampart very high and measuring four hundred cubits 
in length. It was twenty-five cubits broad. It was beautifully constructed 
with gems, scarlet like vermilion. Outside the rampart, he attached to the 
wall two gates of gem; and inside the rampart, he constructed seven gates and 
thus blocked up all the moats. With rubies he constructed for the mansion 
twenty four square-courts and attached to them columns of sandal, With 
gems resembling in hue the saffron, he constructed stair-cases : to the roof of 
the mansion, he attached jars of gem, of various colours ‘Ihe golden jars and 
the portals of diamond added considerably to the splendour of the upper story 
of the house. le then proceeded to construct lovely royal roads which he 
decked on all sides with platforms of ruby and, on their right and left sides, with 
splendid houses of gem fit for merchandise. ‘Then he went to the grove of 
Vrindávana wherein he constructed the sphere of the Risa with a beautiful 
Circular rampart of gem. Hound the sphere he constructed a platform with 
gems, which was one yojan in circumference, Within that sphere he con-tructed

king be born like unto him. After a while, the king, according to the counsel of

celebrated by the name of Kedfra. Upto this day, if any body dies there,

digit of Kamala, devoted to the deity and expert in abstract meditations, 
Vrind& did not marry. ‘The great devotee Durvásá gave her the unattainable 
mystic formula (mantra) of Hari. ‘lhen with a stoic apathy or indifference she 
renounced the world, went to the fcrest and there in a solitary place she prayed
