205-219. The merciful Lord appeared and cheerfully offered a boon to 
her. Vrind& was excited with lust at the sight of the lovely features of the 
Supreme Being and fainted Sho thensolicited a boon to the following effect, 
“Lord, be my husband." Krişņa complied and enjoyed her society for several 
years. Jhen Vrindá went cheerfully with the Lord to the Goloka and was 
there regarded as the foremost of the fortunate cowherdesses. That place 
where she dallied with Krisna or where she prayed God was called Vrinda- 
vana. Listen to other sacred anecdotes furnishing an explanation of the above 
name. In days of yore, a king named Kusadhwaja had two daughters named 
Tulsi and Vedavati. They were well versed in religious books and having 
renounced the world they gave themselves up to meditations. Afterwards 
Vedavati obtained Narayana as her husband. She is every where known by 
the name of Sita, the daughter of Janaka. Tulsi, also desirous to get Hari 
as her husband, prayed God ; but accidentally through the curse of Durvásàá, 
she was married first to the demcn called S’ankha and eventually to Hari. 
This ‘Lulsi was turned intoa tree by the curse of Hari, aud Hari was turned 
into the Holy Stone by the curse of lulsi. This beautiful Tulsî always 
resides in the heart of Hari who hus assumed the image of a holy stone. I al- 
ready narrated to you in detail the account of ‘Lulst; and now incidentally 
I have again referred to that subject, ‘Tulsi is synonymous with Vrinda. 
She, too, prayed Hari at that place. Hence it is called Vrindávana. But 
there are other causes, too, why it is so called. Vrind& is one of the sixteen 
names of Rádhà, Radha was engaged in this place in her amorous pastimes. 
Hence it is so called.

Kántá and S/ata-Chandra-Nibhánaná, Rå means gift and Dhá means emancipa- 
tion. She who gives emancipation is Râdhâ. She is the wife of the lord of the 
Rasa, hence Ktase$wari. She dwells in the sphere of the Rasa, hence she is Râs- 
básini, She is pre-eminently the chief of jovial and witty goddesses, hence 
Rasikeswart. She is dearer to Krisna than his life, hence Krigna-Pranadhika. 
She is the favourite consort of Krisna, hence Krisna-Priy& ; she can bestow 
on her votaries the form of Krisna and is equal to Krisna in every respect, 
hence she is called Krisna-Swarupini. 


234-245, She sprang out of the left side of Krigna, hence she is called 
Krisna-bàám&msa-Sambhütá, She is all-joy, happiness incarnate, hence 
Paramananda-rupini. She gives the best kind of redemption ; hence she is 
called Krisnà. She has got a Vrindávana, or she is the presiding deity of 
Vrindávana ; hence she is culled  Vrindávani. Vrinda means -female 
associates and A means exist. She has got female associates, hence she is called 
Vrindá, Vinod means pleasure ; she gets pleasure at Vrindávana ; hence she is 
called Vrindávana-vinodini, Her face and nails shine like the moon, hence 
she is styled Chandrávati by Krisna. The grace of her body is always like 
that of the moon ; hence she has been cheerfully named as Ohandia-K&ntá by 
Hari. Her features have the charms of a hundred moons ; hence she is called 
S'ata-Chandra Nibhánanà. Thus I related to you her sixteen names with their 
etymology and meanings. Hari recited them to Brahma, while the latter was 
residing in the lotus of bis navel. Afterwards, Brahma recited them to my 
father Dharma. Later on, on the occasion of the solar festival, in the 
assembly of the gods, at the great shrine of the Puskara, I raised the question 
of Radh&’s prowess. "Then Dharma was graciously pleased to give them 
(i. e., the etymology and meaning of her names) to me. O great saint, 1 just 
recited to you the holy hymn containing the sixteen names of Radha. 


246-251. Whoever throughout his life reads the above hymn in the * 
morning, noon and evening every day attains faith in the lotus-feet of Radha i 
and Mádhava and in the end secures all kinds of perfection, viz., Animá (an | 
esoteric subtle state, a faculty acquired by practice which enables a man to a 
assume atomic or invisible form) etc. He wieldsa body which never perishes 4 
and, engaged in the service of Radha and Krişna, passes his days merrily in 2m 
their company. The perusal of this hymn is sixteen times more beneficial, i 
than any of the following acts : the due celebration of all the rites, gift, fasting, 
the study of the Vedas with their commentaries, the due performance of all 
kinds of sacrifice, all sorts of pilgrimage or a journey to all holy shrines, seyen- 
fold ciroumambulation round the globe, the protection of fugitives. tha educa- — 
tion of the ignorant, the sigbt of the gods or the Vaignavas. Nay,a person, 
by virtue of this hymn, is redeemed in his life-time. usd

saw the city which wss superior to heaven, they exclaimed, * Oh! what a 
wonder ! what a wonder !” One said to the other, ‘‘Who has constructed all 
this? Is there such a powerful being in the world ?" Then Nanda remember- 
ed the words of Garga and knew in his mind that the whole world containing 
animate and inaminate objects, from Brahma down to a bush, can be created 
or destroyed by the will of god, by a mere twinkle of his eye. There is 
nothing impossible with Hari whocontrols even that Mahá-Visnu sustaining a 
universe on each of the pores of his skin, whose lotus feet are constantly adored 
by Brahma, Visnu, Mahesa and Dharma, who has assumed the form of a man 
by virtue of his illusions. With this predominant idea, Nanda, the lord of the 
cow-herds, wandered, here and there, saw the housesand perused the name of 
the owner inscribed on the wall of each house. He allotted to every one the 
house meant for him. Then Nanda and Vrisabhánu, with their heart beating with 
curiosity entered into the house with their relations at an auspicious moment. 
The residents of Vrindfivana entered into their respective dwelling houses ; 
and their faces and eyes beamed forth with joy. They were all pleased with 
the places assigned to them. Thus I gave you an account of the construction 
of the city. The boys sported there merrily ; and Krisna and Baladeva took 
part in amusements along with the children in different places of the sphere of 
the Rasa and in the lovely groves.
